#3098. If the Nasdaq's Correction Turns Into a Bear Market, This Is the First Stock I'll Buy

Another 7.5% fall would send the index into a bear market. Which battered giant would I buy first?

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 20, 2026 at 10:57PM EST

The Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -5.28%) finished at 23,879 on Thursday, Nov. 19. The tech-heavy index now sits about 13.5% below its Oct. 6 record close of 27,600 -- a correction, though not a bear market.

The standard line for a bear market is a 20% fall from the high. For the Nasdaq, that's a close near 22,080, or about another 7.5% drop from Thursday's level.

I'm not predicting the index gets there. But a plan is easier to stick to when it's written down before a sell-off than during one, and mine is this: If the Nasdaq closes in bear-market territory, the first stock I'll buy is Alphabet (GOOGL -2.70%)(GOOG -2.59%).

Shares of the Google parent closed at $272.66 on Thursday, their lowest close since October 2025. The stock's now about 32% below where it finished the day on May 13, at a record close of $402.62 -- more than double the Nasdaq's decline from its own high.

A lot of that drop came in a single session. The stock fell about 13% on Oct. 29, the day after Alphabet posted third-quarter results showing slower Search growth, lower earnings per share, and a second straight quarter of negative free cash flow.

In other words, the stock's arguably already taken the sort of hit a bear market brings, while the business underneath keeps growing.

Showing how well the business has held up through the drop, Alphabet's revenue growth has accelerated all year. Sales grew 22% year over year in the first quarter, then 24% in the second, and third-quarter sales rose 25% to $127.6 billion. Operating income grew 30% year over year in all three quarters. And in the first nine months of 2026, operating income came to $121 billion, a 30% jump from $93 billion a year before.

I focus on operating income because Alphabet's net income swings with the value of its stakes in other companies. Other income (mainly investment gains) added around $127 billion in the same nine months.

That said, not every number is heading the right way. Last year's third quarter had a $3.5 billion European Commission fine, so without it, operating income rose more like 17% in the latest quarter. And as spending on artificial intelligence (AI) shows up in costs, the operating margin has shrunk. It was 31.8% in the third quarter, versus 34% in the April-to-June period and 36.1% in the first quarter.

The second reason is the balance sheet. Alphabet expects to spend between $195 billion and $205 billion on capital expenditures in 2026, and management said that amount is expected to rise significantly in 2027. A bear market could make raising money for that sort of spending more expensive.

But Alphabet has already raised a lot of it. In the first nine months of 2026, the company raised about $85 billion in new debt, about $43 billion by selling common stock, and another $19 billion by issuing preferred stock.

Alphabet's cash and marketable securities totaled $265.1 billion at the end of September, more than twice its total at the end of 2025. Long-term debt was $125.1 billion.

Yes, selling new shares dilutes current shareholders, and free cash flow was negative in both of the past two quarters. But with around $140 billion more in cash and marketable securities than in long-term debt, Alphabet arguably doesn't need a steady stock market to keep funding its build-out.

Alphabet stock doesn't look expensive to me now. At $272.66, it costs around 18 times expected 2027 earnings, while Meta Platforms, another ad-driven tech giant, trades at about 17 times its expected 2027 earnings. For a business growing operating income by double digits, I think the price is fair.

I wouldn't argue with anyone buying some now. The reason to wait for the line is that a bear market would probably mark down many stocks at once. Deciding beforehand which one gets the first dollar stops me from chasing whatever happened to fall the most that week.

Alphabet doesn't have to track the index, either. But if it just matched the Nasdaq's remaining 7.5% drop from Thursday's close, the stock would trade near $252, or about 17 times expected 2027 earnings.

If the Nasdaq closes 20% below its record, Alphabet is likely where my plan begins.

Sure, the index might never get there. And if it doesn't, Alphabet at around 18 times expected 2027 earnings still looks fairly priced to me.

#3095. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#3089. Follow the Smart Money: 4 AI Stocks Billionaire Dan Loeb Just Bought

Hedge fund manager Dan Loeb was buying shares of Nvidia, Space Exploration Technologies, Microsoft, and Meta Platforms in Q3 2026.

By Geoffrey Seiler – Nov 21, 2026 at 12:00AM EST

Dan Loeb is widely considered one of the world's top investors, starting his Third Point hedge fund with a few million dollars in 1995 and turning it into an over-$28 billion behemoth. In Q3 2026, he was busy scooping up several artificial intelligence (AI) stocks, including Nvidia (NVDA -14.01%), Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX -7.69%), Microsoft (MSFT -2.77%), and Meta Platforms (META -5.93%).

Let's look at what may have attracted Loeb to these names.

Loeb's buy of Nvidia shares came before CEO Jensen Huang stepped down, but the company remains in the pole position in the AI infrastructure build-out. Nvidia today is more than just a graphics processing unit (GPU) provider, offering a complete AI factory solution that includes networking, central processing units (CPUs), language processing units (LPUs) for the decode phase of inference, its Nemotron open-weight models, and an expanding software layer.

That package has helped Nvidia increase its revenue per gigawatt from around $18 billion when it introduced its Hopper architecture a few years ago to around $40 billion today with Vera Rubin.

With future memory and manufacturing capacity locked in, along with its strong ecosystem, expect Nvidia to remain the AI infrastructure leader over the coming years.

SpaceX is one of the most exciting companies on the planet as it looks to disrupt industries and conquer space. The company recently achieved a major milestone by catching the upper stage of its Starship rocket, setting it up to fundamentally change the economics of space launches as it moves toward rapid reuse. This is also a stepping stone for SpaceX to hit some of its loftier goals, such as orbital data centers, Earth-to-Earth transport, and asteroid mining.

Back on Earth, SpaceX has become one of the world's largest neocloud companies and plans to rapidly grow its AI infrastructure-as-a-service business. Right now, it is getting great rates on its AI infrastructure through short-term rentals, allowing it to see a payback on its investments within a year. The company also plans to disrupt the U.S. mobile market, and its Cursor AI coding platform is growing quickly.

Microsoft continues to see strong growth in both its Azure cloud computing unit and its enterprise software business. Azure has been a persistent 30%-plus revenue grower, with its revenue growth hitting 48% last quarter. With significant commitments from OpenAI and an overall $677 billion backlog, Azure should remain a strong growth driver in the years ahead.

At the same time, the company has been gaining significant traction with its AI Copilot assistants across Microsoft 365 and its GitHub code repository. The shift to a seat-plus-consumption model has been paying off, and Copilot usage has been surging, with it seeing an eightfold year-over-year increase in seats last quarter. Given how embedded the company's software and governance tools are in the enterprise space, it looks well positioned to emerge as an AI software-as-a-service (SaaS) winner.

Meta Platforms has been one of the best companies at using AI to drive growth in an already established, strong business. It's employing AI to both keep users on its apps longer by displaying more content they are interested in and to feed them more relevant ads. This is leading to higher ad loads, stronger conversions, and better ad pricing.

Last quarter, its revenue surged 26%, with a 14% increase in average price per ad and a 10% jump in ad impressions.

Perhaps more exciting, though, is that Meta is jumping headfirst into consumer AI agents. These agents can help users with a multitude of tasks, but Meta will benefit most when they make purchases on a user's behalf. Here, Meta will get a cut of the transaction fee, giving it another potential new large revenue stream and putting it at the forefront of agentic commerce.

#3076. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#3074. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#3071. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#3064. Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#3063. ‘It worked every time': How Sixers found a clutch answer at shootaround

'It worked every time': How Sixers found a clutch answer at shootaround originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers created their two cleanest late-game looks several hours before they needed them.

During Wednesday morning's shootaround in Orlando, Nick Nurse had the Sixers work on offensive material they had not consistently used this season. They tested one of those actions during the first half, returned to it throughout the closing minutes and watched it produce the shots they needed to complete a 17-point comeback.

"We worked on some stuff this morning that we hadn't used before, and they ran all that stuff very, very well and very calmly and with composure," Nurse told reporters.

The Sixers have earned six of their 13 wins after trailing by double digits. They are also 11-0 in games that reached clutch time.

Relying on comebacks is not a sustainable plan. The repeated late-game situations, however, have forced a new roster to respond to different lineups, coverages and responsibilities while the season is still young.

Each close finish gives Nurse more information about which players and actions hold up when possessions tighten. Wednesday gave him a new option.

Anfernee Simons' clinching three in the Sixers' 121-116 win over the Magic was the final result of that preparation.

"That last play, we ran that play in the first half," Simons said. "We worked it a couple of times, and in the second half we ran that play every single time down the stretch, and it worked every time.

"It was a play we ran through. [It's gratifying] especially when it's working consistently and you're getting different shots out of it and the opposing team just don't know what to do.

"We just continue to run the play and get the open shots we need."

NBC Sports Philadelphia video producer and Sixers Talk podcast co-host Kevin Rice broke down how the Sixers used its different options to keep Orlando guessing.

The action begins with two players stationed near the elbows. A player on the opposite elbow then sets a screen for the player up top, who receives the ball while moving around it.

The Sixers ran the set three consecutive times during the first half. None of the possessions reached the primary option. Each still produced a quality opportunity.

On the first, Joel Embiid cleared LeBron James out of the area and directed Simons around him for a dribble handoff. Simons received an open three-point look.

Orlando took away the handoff the next time down but left Embiid one-on-one against Wendell Carter Jr. Embiid created enough room to retrieve his own miss and convert an and-one opportunity.

The third attempt pulled Orlando's defense into the paint. Two passes moved the ball through the collapsing coverage and created an open three for VJ Edgecombe.

"We had a little thing where he comes flying off behind Joel, and he doesn't get to do that that much, right?" Nurse said of Simons. "So tonight he gets the opportunity to do it, and it was very good for us on a number of plays."

The Sixers changed the action late without abandoning its initial Horns alignment. Simons moved through the lane to help form a Spain pick-and-roll involving James and Embiid.

On the first late possession, Simons remained near the play after James gained a matchup against Carter. Orlando struggled to sort out its assignments, leaving James open for a 19-foot jumper with 54.8 seconds remaining.

After Jalen Suggs made one of two free throws, the Sixers returned to the same setup. This time, Simons relocated to the left corner after James drew Carter on a switch.

James drove into the paint. Suggs lost track of Simons while responding to the penetration, and James sent the ball toward the corner.

Simons buried the open three to give the Sixers a 119-113 lead with 15 seconds left.

"Bron just penetrated the paint," Simons said. "It kind of got some confusion, and when I was on the dunker and kind of spaced out to the corner, I ended up finding myself wide open for the three, and Bron found me."

The Sixers entered the night without Tyrese Maxey and Jaylen Brown, their two leading scorers. Simons made his first start with Philadelphia and recorded 18 points in the city where he grew up.

His final basket also illustrated why Nurse believes the Sixers have more to uncover with him.

"When he's out there, they gotta be really careful," Nurse said. "So I think there's some drives he creates, and just about every time he lets it go, whether he's on balance or not, you think it's going in, and that is a big deal. Like, that is a big deal.

"And again, I think we're still a ways away from really unlocking him to being even more part of some of the things we can do with him to create more shots."

Philadelphia is now 11-0 in clutch games. The finish in Orlando provided a more specific explanation than experience or shot-making alone.

The Sixers installed a set in the morning, explored its counters and trusted it when the game tightened. James supplied the patience to read Orlando's coverage. Embiid occupied the middle of the action. Simons moved without the ball, and Edgecombe remained ready to shoot.

James said his heart rate drops when close games increase the pressure on everyone else.

"I mean, experience," James said. "A guy that's seen every situation this league has to offer. It's nothing I haven't seen. And, you know, in the moments where people's heart rate goes up or anxiety goes up, mine is pretty low. It's actually lower, you know."

The Sixers still have more actions and reads to learn as Nurse becomes familiar with his new personnel, but their latest win showed their innate ability to adapt in big moments.

"I think we're still a long ways away from knowing all the reads and all that stuff because it's just a little early, right?" Nurse said.

→ Baca artikel asli

#3051. How LeBron James dissected Pacers defense down the stretch

How LeBron James dissected Pacers defense down the stretch originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

As he overtook Jason Kidd for the third-most assists in NBA history, LeBron James piloted the Sixers' offense down the stretch against the Indiana Pacers on Sunday.

With Joel Embiid sitting out and Tyrese Maxey getting a breather on the bench, James took over in the fourth quarter. He did so by spamming the same action over and over again.

The set consisted of James getting a ball screen from a guard – either VJ Edgecombe or Anfernee Simons – at the top of the key. After setting or ghosting the screen, the guard would short roll towards the basket.

When the Pacers blitzed or hedged the screen, James found the guard. The Sixers could then attack a 4-on-3 or 3-on-2 situation.

Twice, Edgecombe and Simons scored off the roll.

When the Pacers helped to the roller, that opened up the shooters in each corner.

James directed traffic on offense to ensure that the Sixers spaced the floor properly. Here, he waved Dean Wade out of the dunker spot and into the corner. Edgecombe promptly hit him for an open three off the short roll.

After all of the Sixers' success, the Pacers eventually switched the ball screen. That left the 6-foot-4, 173-pound Quenton Jackson on James, who has five inches and 77 pounds on him.

Jackson and James had already gone head to head on Sunday.

In the first quarter, James set a ball screen for Edgecombe which the Pacers switched. Edgecombe fed James in the post and the Pacers doubled him to support Jackson. James punished them for it.

They didn't double James after the switch in the fourth quarter. This time, James backed Jackson down, backed him down again and backed him down some more before scoring inside.

James' strength abusing matchups against smaller defenders after switches has been a safety valve for the Sixers' offense. Guards struggle to stop him from getting to the basket. When defenses send help, he picks them apart with his otherworldly passing ability.

That combination forced the Pacers to pick their poison. From the 9:55 mark in the fourth until the Pacers switched to a zone defense (after James bullied Jackson) with just over 6:00 left, the Sixers scored 13 points out of this action across seven possessions. That's a stellar 1.86 points per possession.

James, who's scoring a career-low 15.1 points per game so far this season, might not be the elite bucket-getter he was for most of his career. But he's still brilliant at conducting highly efficient offense that pressures a defense's pain points.

#3050. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#3038. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#3036. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#3035. The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats!

The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats! originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

This 76ers team is a heck of a lot of fun to watch and fun basketball means fun stats!

At 13-3, the 76ers are exactly a month into the 5th-best start in franchise history and best since they were 14-2 in 2000-01. Which turned out to be a pretty good year. They were 15-1 in 1949-50 and 1966-67 and 14-2 in 1981-82.

To help everybody get through this interminable two-day gap between 76ers games, here's our first 2026-27 installment of Roob's 76ers Stats, with a tip of the hat to Stathead.

→ The 76ers have been out-rebounded by 34 through 16 games (719-685) and their 13-3 record matches the best in NBA history by a team that's been out-rebounded after 16 games. Three other teams have been 13-3 despite getting out-rebounded, most recently the 2013-14 Miami Heat. The others are the 1979-80 Bucks and 2012-2013 Spurs. The 76ers are an NBA-best 6-3 this year when they've been out-rebounded (which means they're 7-0 when they have a rebounding edge).

→ In the win over Orlando Thursday, V.J. Edgecombe had 24 points, eight rebounds, six assists and two steals, and Joel Embiid had 26 points, six rebounds, seven assists and two steals. It was the first time two 76ers had at least 24 points, six rebounds and assists and two steals in a game in 53 years. In a win over the Celtics at the Spectrum in 1973, Fred Carter had 31 points, six rebounds, six assists and seven steals and George McGinniss had 28 points, 16 rebounds, eight assists and three steals.

→ Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, a career 37 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 44 percent so far. Anfernee Simons, a career 38 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 41 percent. Jabari Walker, a career 33 percent 3-point shooter, is at 42 percent. Dean Wade, a career 37 percent shooter, is at 41 percent. Jaylen Brown, a career 36 percent shooter from range, is at 39 percent. The 76ers are shooting just 36 percent as a team from the stripe because James and Embiid have been so bad, but everybody else is off to career-best starts from 3-point range.

→ The 76ers are 7-1 on the road, equalling their best road record ever after eight games. They were also 7-1 in 1980-81, 1981-82 and 1982-83. The 1981 team had the best road start in franchise history, going 12-1 before losing its second road game.

→ With 15.7 points, 6.8 assists and 6.8 rebounds per game, LeBron James is only the fifth 76er ever to average at least 15 ½ points and 6 ½ assists and rebounds through 16 games. The others are some familiar names: Wilt Chamberlain in 1967, Charles Barkley in 1987, Ben Simmons in 2018 and James Harden in 2023.

→ The 76ers already have more wins this year after trailing by 10 points through the first quarter than all but two full seasons since 1950. They had four in 1985-86 and again in 2022-23. The Hawks also have three wins this year when trailing by double digits after the first quarter, but they're 3-4 and the 76ers are 3-2. So they actually have a winning record when they're down by double digits going into the second quarter.

→ With Jaylen Brown (22.6, 6.6), Joel Embiid (20.0, 8.3), LeBron James (15.7, 6.8) and V.J. Edgecombe (13.6, 6.5), the 76ers are the only NBA team with four players averaging 6 ½ rebounds and 13 points. The last time they had four players in a season who averaged 13 points and 6 ½ rebounds was 1961-62 when they were the Syracuse Nationals, with Dave Gambee (16.7, 7.9), Hal Greer (22.8, 7.4), Red Kerr (16.3, 14.7), Dolph Schayes (14.7, 7.8) and Lee Shaffer (16.9, 6.8).

→ Edgecombe's 6.5 rebounds per game are 2nd-most among shooting guards behind only the Grizzlies' Cedric Coward, who is at 8.5. Edgecombe's 6.5 is also 2nd-highest ever by a 76ers shooting guard behind only Greer's 7.4 during that 1961-62 season. Edgecombe averaged 5.6 rebounds as a rookie and his 5.8 career average is highest by a shooting guard who spent more than one year with the 76ers. Greer is second at 5.3. Among all NBA players who've played at least 90 games, Edgecombe's 5.8 average is 11th-highest all-time among shooting guards (although Stathead includes some swing guard-forwards in their data).

→ Joel Embiid's 14.6 shooting percentage from 3 is worst in NBA history after 16 games (minimum 40 attempts). Embiid is 6-for-41 from the stripe this year. The previous 3-point shooting low 16 games into a season belonged to Cedric Ceballos of the Lakers in 1995-96 and the 76ers' Evan Turner in 2013-14. Both were 6-for-43 for 16.3 percent.

→ LeBron James is at 22.2 percent from 3 on 10-for-45. The Sixers are the first NBA team in 12 years with two players under 23 percent after 16 games with a minimum of 40 attempts. In 2014 Tony Wroten was 10-for-50 (10 percent) and Turner was 6-for-43 (16.3 percent).

#3034. Some FHA borrowers pay mortgage insurance for a decade or more. There's an industry push to change the rules

Homeowners with conventional loans who pay mortgage insurance typically can shed that expense — meant to protect a lender against loss if the borrower defaults — once they've built sufficient equity in their home. But that's not how it works for those with a Federal Housing Administration loan.

A fresh push to change the rules governing mortgage insurance for FHA loans came this week, after the Department of Housing and Urban Development released a report to Congress on Monday showing that the FHA's mortgage insurance fund remains financially strong. Its capital ratio — a measure of its financial cushion against potential losses — was 10.29% as of Sept. 30, down from 11.47% a year earlier, but still more than five times the 2% minimum required by Congress.

"The program's financial strength provides an opportunity for targeted changes that improve affordability for borrowers while preserving the long-term stability" of FHA's insurance fund, said Bob Broeksmit, president and CEO of the Mortgage Bankers Association, in a statement released after the report was issued. The trade group was one of a handful of organizations that put out statements calling for changes.

The push, which revives a longstanding mortgage industry request, also comes several weeks after President Donald Trump issued an executive order that included calling for the launch of a federal pilot program that would cancel mortgage insurance on certain conventional loans once borrowers meet equity requirements.

The order would apply generally to future loans backed by Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac — two government-sponsored enterprises that purchase qualifying mortgages from lenders and resell them as bundled securities. It wouldn't apply to FHA loans.

FHA mortgages are issued by private lenders but insured by the federal government, which protects lenders against losses if a borrower defaults. The loans are designed to make homeownership more accessible, particularly for borrowers with limited savings for a down payment or with lower credit scores.

Affordability challenges persist in the housing market, with elevated home prices and mortgage rates putting homeownership out of reach for many prospective buyers. Existing homeowners are also facing rising costs alongside their mortgages, including property taxes and homeowners insurance amid overall high inflation.

About 83% of new FHA purchase loans went to first-time homebuyers in the most recent fiscal year, according to the FHA report to Congress. Altogether, the FHA insured more than 8.4 million mortgages as of Sept. 30.

The median age of first-time buyers is 40, according to a new report from the National Association of Realtors.

FHA loans also require borrowers to pay mortgage insurance regardless of the size of their down payment. Under current rules established in 2013, FHA borrowers with a down payment that's less than 10% of the loan must pay mortgage insurance premiums for the duration of the loan. Those who put 10% or more down pay that insurance for 11 years.

Borrowers generally pay both an upfront mortgage insurance premium and an annual premium, which is typically divided into monthly payments. The upfront premium is usually 1.75% of the base loan amount and can be financed into the mortgage. For many borrowers with 30-year FHA loans, the annual premium is 0.55% of the outstanding loan balance.

Conventional mortgages generally don't require mortgage insurance unless the loan is for more than 80% of the home's value. In 2022, the premium typically ranged from 0.58% to 1.86% annually, according to Fannie Mae.

If those borrowers have to pay mortgage insurance, they can typically request its removal once they've built 20% equity in the home, or it is automatically canceled when their loan drops to 78% of the original value.

The mortgage industry has floated two different proposals.

One would reduce the rate that applies to FHA mortgage insurance. For example, the Mortgage Bankers Association recently suggested a rate reduction of 20 basis points, which would lower the standard 0.55% rate to 0.35% for new borrowers.

For example: On a $350,000 mortgage, a 0.55% rate generally translates into $1,925 annually, or about $160 monthly, at the start of the loan. As the principal owed slowly goes down over time through a borrower's monthly payments, so does that mortgage insurance amount.

At 0.35%, the annual premium would be $1,225 at the start of the loan, or $102 monthly — a difference of $58 per month.

The FHA last cut that rate in 2023, when it was reduced from 0.85%. At the time, HUD estimated that affected borrowers would save an average of $800 annually. However, in the FHA's new report to Congress, the agency noted that the 2023 cut has reduced the fund's revenue, contributing to a decline in its capital ratio.

The other industry proposal would implement auto-cancellation of premiums once the loan balance fell to 78% of the home's original value at purchase — in other words, the same as for Fannie- and Freddie-backed mortgages.

"Cancellation prevents the squeeze for borrowers who have paid down their loans and shouldn't be paying for coverage they have outgrown," said Michael Farrell, president of the National Association of Mortgage Brokers, a trade group that generally has supported the cancellation proposal.

"NAMB believes that cancellation at 78% protects the [FHA insurance fund], because premiums can restart if the [capital] ratio ever falls to 2%," Farrell said.

FHA mortgages received that treatment from 2001 through 2013, but the agency eliminated it to shore up its insurance fund after financial reserves deteriorated over time following the housing market crash, which led to the 2008 financial crisis.

Whether the proposals will go anywhere this time is uncertain. HUD, which oversees the FHA, did not respond to a CNBC email seeking more information.

#3032. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

#3029. White House replaces cyber director Sean Cairncross amid widespread tech outages

White House National Cyber Director Sean Cairncross is leaving his post, with Pentagon CIO Kirsten Davies set to serve as acting director after a week of high-profile U.S. technology outages.

The role, which advises the president on cybersecurity and coordinates U.S. cyber policy across federal agencies, has been handed to Davies, the Pentagon's chief information officer, on an interim basis. A permanent national cyber director must first be nominated by the president and then confirmed by the Senate.

Axios first reported Cairncross' exit, citing two White House officials who said President Donald Trump's chief of staff, Stephen Miller, pushed for his departure.

The White House declined to comment. However, a source familiar with the White House's thinking told CNBC that the President fully supported firing Sean Cairncross from his position because he lacked the strong cyber credentials to perform the job. Davies has the credentials to excel in the position and has been Senate-confirmed, the person added.

The Office of the National Cyber Director did not immediately respond to a CNBC request for comment.

It comes after a series of technology outages, including a nationwide aviation ground stop, a worldwide Microsoft Outlook outage and a reported blackout of the National Security Agency's classified communications network.

The White House has insisted that there are no indications of a coordinated cyberattack, state-sponsored activity or malign actor behind recent tech outages.

Davies addressed her responsibilities in the new position and thanked Miller and Trump for their confidence late Thursday.

"Our nation stands in the crossroad of analog, digital, and quantum technologies, our American way of living increasingly interconnected. Our opportunity is to dominate the cyber landscape for generations to come … but only if we modernize and drive resilience into the foundations our great nation runs on," she wrote on X.

Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth and Secretary of State Marco Rubio both congratulated Davies on her appointment.

#3028. Observations after Sixers come back for strong shorthanded victory

Observations after Sixers come back for strong shorthanded victory originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stretched their winning streak to four games and snagged a strong road win Wednesday night over the Magic.

They earned a 121-116 victory in Orlando to improve to 13-3 on the season. The Magic dropped to 6-8.

Joel Embiid had 26 points, seven assists, six rebounds, three blocks and two steals.

VJ Edgecombe posted a season-high 24 points, eight boards and six assists.

LeBron James scored 22 points and Anfernee Simons added 18.

Magic star Franz Wagner had 27 points, 11 assists and eight rebounds.

The Sixers had a long injury list in Orlando. Tyrese Maxey (right foot soreness), Jaylen Brown (left hamstring strain) and Dean Wade (left hamstring soreness/tightness) were among their players out. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse told reporters in Orlando that Brown will be re-evaluated when the team returns to Philadelphia.

Orlando was without star forward Paolo Banchero (right high ankle sprain).

The Sixers will start a three-game homestand when they face the Bucks on Saturday afternoon. Here are observations on their win over the Magic:

The Sixers' undermanned starting lineup was Edgecombe, Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, James and Embiid.

After scoring 37 points in the Sixers' win over the Nets, Embiid had a subpar start. He began 1 for 6 from the floor and committed a turnover that led to an Anthony Black dunk.

The Sixers' lineup looked exactly like a unit that hadn't built any chemistry yet. The team was not sharp early on either end of the floor.

Desmond Bane drained a long three-pointer to give Orlando a 27-12 advantage. Orlando made 11 of its first 14 field goals and led by as many as 17 points in the first quarter.

Labaron Philon Jr. and Justin Edwards joined the Sixers' rotation. So did two-way contract players Tacko Fall and Caleb Love.

Philon impressed in the first quarter, converting two floaters and a corner three. The rookie passed his previous NBA scoring high of five points and finished the night with 10 on 4-for-5 shooting.

Fall played 10 minutes and posted two points, three rebounds and three blocks. He slammed in a dunk and rejected two shots early in the second quarter. Credit to Fall for giving the Sixers a good backup center performance and doing his job as a rim-protecting ultra-big man.

Love entered in the second quarter and was ready to fire, hitting a catch-and-shoot three-pointer on his first touch.

The Sixers' zone defense also helped them eat into the Magic's lead. The zone seemed to disrupt Orlando's offensive rhythm and it was active enough to prevent the Magic from consistently creating high-quality looks.

Edgecombe's buzzer-beating mid-range jumper cut the Sixers' deficit to 69-64 after two quarters.

The Sixers pulled within a single point on multiple occasions in the third quarter, including on a Philon three late in the period. They trailed by five points after the third.

Fall continued to play well early in the fourth quarter. He deterred the Magic's drivers from getting all the way to the rim as the very large back line of the Sixers' zone defense and made eye-catching hustle plays on both sides of the ball.

The Sixers outscored Orlando by seven points on the night with Fall on the court. Soon after Embiid subbed back in, he evened the game at 104-all with two free throws. Jabari Walker swished a three to finally push the Sixers in front.

The Magic regained a late lead with a Jalen Suggs three. James then drove and kicked the ball out to Edgecombe, who nailed one of his customary clutch jumpers to put the Sixers back on top.

No matter the night-to-night personnel, the Sixers have been fantastic at the tail end of close games. They're now 11-0 in clutch situations, which the NBA defines as games within a five-point differential in the final five minutes.

#3025. Bitcoin sell-off: Crypto is failing the 'digital gold' test again as it sinks with stocks

Bitcoin's latest sell-off is reviving doubts that it can behave as the "digital gold" market hedge that crypto backers have long envisioned.

Coming into 2026, experts said that showing bitcoin could serve as a market hedge rather than a risk-on asset that moves up and down with growth stocks was an important test for the cryptocurrency.

Bitcoin was trading under $62,000 on Friday morning, down from an all-time high of more than $126,000 in early October last year. Its downward spiral in recent months and weeks has been precipitated by a confluence of factors. The heavy midterm elections losses for the Republicans, the Binance hack, and a world on edge about the security of all things technology — FAA ground stop, the outage of the National Security Agency's highly classified internal communications system, several online banking outages — have not helped the mood when it comes to putting trust in the digital world.

But the market itself may be the biggest factor. Investors are currently in sell everything mode, including tech stocks and gold — the Dow Jones Industrial Average and S&P 500 tumbled into correction territory on Friday, according to CNBC data. Bitcoin's recent price action has also tracked the move in the biggest tech IPOs, SpaceX and Anthropic. Taken together, the cryptocurrency is failing the test again when it comes to showing its worth as a market hedge during times of investor anxiety.

In the past month alone, bitcoin had dropped roughly $20,000, as of Friday morning, according to CNBC data.

Investors and financial management firms aren't viewing it as safe an investment as in the past, said Ryan Kirkley, co-founder and chief executive of Global Settlement Network, a Miami-based fintech that provides infrastructure for currency settlement, including crypto. "There's a bit more caution than there was a year ago."

What is behind bitcoin's fall, and what is in store for its future? Here is what experts say.

There have been many theories about how bitcoin would react based on different market and economic conditions. But historically, bitcoin has done best in low-rate environments and when lending opportunities are plentiful, said Juan Xavier Sanchez, head of wealth strategy at Activest Wealth Management in Aventura, Florida.

When rates are down and treasuries aren't paying good yields, people tend to be more risk-seeking for higher returns. "Bitcoin for many investors is in that bucket," Sanchez said.

Experts have noted that gold, which is now down over 35% from its early 2026 peak, is less attractive when cash accounts are paying 5%. Investments like gold and bitcoin offer no yield.

Bitcoin's rising correlation with the Nasdaq-100 since late September suggests the two are increasingly moving in tandem, according to data from Fairlead Strategies, a technical research firm. That's consistent with an environment of higher volatility in which correlations across risk assets tend to increase. However, the correlation remains below its early-2026 highs, the data show.

Bitcoin has certain characteristics of digital gold, in that it has a limited supply, no single owner and no CEO or board that can decide to issue more, said Anthony Georgiades, founder and general partner at Innovating Capital in New York, which invests in early-stage crypto, blockchain, digital infrastructure and deep tech companies.

But it's also been caught up in monetary policy since its inception. The last decade or so of monetary policy has been extremely rewarding to speculative risk-on assets, Georgiades said. However, in periods of volatility, those types of assets have experienced the most significant drawdowns and have been the most heavily punished, Georgiades said. That means that a shock on one side of the board that might be completely independent of bitcoin could trigger a large volatility event "given how intertwined digital assets have become in the overall financial system," he said.

Notably, bitcoin hasn't acted much like digital gold in more recent years, said Abe Sheikh, chief investment officer of Cordoba Advisory Partners in New York. In fact, it has fallen substantially during periods when the stock market has been falling. For example, from October 6 through November 18, the S&P 500 fell about 6.3%, and bitcoin fell around 27%. From January 21, 2025 through April 8, 2025, the S&P dropped around 17.4%, while bitcoin slid 27.8%, he said.

Democrats gaining control over both houses is viewed as a negative for crypto from a market perspective. The crypto industry spent more than $200 million to support crypto-friendly candidates, but its role had less of an impact on overall results than in the last election cycle. However, bitcoin's downward trend was already in the works, said Cyrus Amini, chief investment officer at Hyphen Wealth Management in Lafayette, California.

In September, the Clarity Act failed in the U.S. Senate, and while its outlook is now dim, according to many political and market experts, some form of crypto legislation is still considered a priority for many Democrats and Republicans, while the Trump-appointed heads of the Commodity Future Trading Commission and Securities and Exchange Commission continue to favor crypto in rulemaking.

This fall, investors also started to question the security of encryption technology, leading to more concerns over the security of their bitcoin holdings, said Kirkley of Global Settlement Network. The Binance hack in October muddied the waters further. And the recent stock market jitters and economic concerns piled on doubts, Kirkley said. The last few weeks have been especially rough with data security concerns from multiple fronts, he added.

Wall Street's embrace of bitcoin has been a big positive for the cryptocurrency. Kirkley noted that 20% of bitcoin's market price is coming from institutional investors, and that's a conservative estimate.

In addition, new research from digital assets company Galaxy shows that a record 40.5% of all 13F-reported spot bitcoin exchange-traded product holdings, and 11.2% of all the bitcoin in the funds, came from advisory and wealth planning firms, such as RIAs and brokerage platforms, as of the third quarter 13F filings with the SEC, which are required of firms with over $100 million in assets to report their holdings.

Much of this investment is through bitcoin spot ETFs like the iShares Bitcoin Trust ETF (IBIT) that track its price. These ETFs, first approved by the SEC in January 2024, were supposed to provide a reliable new source of institutional demand. But ETF ownership also makes bitcoin especially easy to sell.

Outflows from several of these funds have been substantial in recent months. IBIT, the largest of its peers, had six-month net outflows of $1.65 billion through Nov. 18 and one-month outflows of $1.37 billion, according to ETF Database. The second biggest, Fidelity Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) had one-month net outflows of $322.65 million and six-month net outflows of $771.86 million, according to ETF Database. Grayscale Bitcoin Mini Trust ETF (BTC) had one-month net outflows of $66.35 million, though it had inflows of $590.04 million over six months.

As of September 2026, public companies collectively hold close to 1.29 million bitcoins, which is more than 6% of the 21 million coins in existence, according to bitcoin.com.

Most public companies invest their corporate treasury portfolios in low-risk, highly liquid instruments such as money market funds, Treasury bonds and Treasury bills. That said, there are some well-known companies that have invested in bitcoin. SpaceX, for example, purchased 18,712 bitcoins for a total cost of $661 million, at an average price of $35,324 per coin. The company began accumulating bitcoin in early 2021 and held its position unchanged from mid-2024 until its IPO filing.

Digital Asset Treasury companies, known as DATs or DATCOs, have also played a role in bitcoin's volatility. DATs are publicly listed firms whose primary business strategy involves holding significant quantities of digital assets, such as bitcoin or ethereum, on their corporate balance sheets to drive shareholder value. Companies in this space include Strategy, Strive, and Twenty One Capital.

"DATs have exacerbated the problem," said Sheikh of Cordoba Advisory Partners. When bitcoin is rising, these companies borrow money in the debt market or raise capital to buy more bitcoin, which magnifies the upswings. They have also sold bitcoin to make their bond coupon payments. "It leads to enormous volatility on the upside and downside," Sheikh said.

As of mid-November, Strategy, the largest corporate holder of bitcoin, holds 850,849 BTC, according to a regulatory filing. That represents roughly 4% of bitcoin's total supply. The company acquired these holdings at a total cost of approximately $64.2 billion, with an average purchase price of roughly $75,452 per coin, according to the filing.

Bitcoin has been trending lower since the peak in October 2025. A trading range has taken hold and the bottom of the range is just below 60,000, said Katie Stockton, founder and managing partner of Fairlead Strategies. "It appears to be trying to find a footing," she said.

Oversold conditions on the heels of recent lows in June and July, in addition to long-term momentum, suggest bitcoin can support an upturn, she said. But that depends on whether the crypto can hold at a current range around $60,000, she said. "At the end of the day, bitcoin is exceptionally volatile. It's still an area that people might go to sell when they think that risk is higher in other areas."

By mid-morning Friday, the price of bitcoin had slipped below $60,000, its lowest mark since July. It was on pace for its seventh-straight weekly loss for the first time since May 2022, according to CNBC data.

#3020. Bitcoin's regulation bull case is played out. What could drive crypto gains moving forward

Crypto investors are hunting for the next positive catalyst after U.S. crypto regulation — a longtime market driver — fizzled in 2026.

Bitcoin traded around $61,500 on Friday, a more than 50% decline from its all-time high above $126,000 reached in October 2025. The broader digital asset market has struggled after the Clarity Act, a crypto market structure bill, failed to advance in the Senate in September.

Investors now see Wall Street's embrace of blockchain technology — particularly tokenization — as the next trend to give bitcoin a boost.

"Every single traditional firm is now looking at digital assets," BitGo CEO Mike Belshe told CNBC last week in an exclusive interview from the fintech-focused Cantor Convergence conference in Miami Beach. That includes BlackRock, the world's largest asset manager.

He pointed to statements from the asset management giant's CEO, Larry Fink, who said the next major evolution in global finance is the creation of digital representations of every stock, bond, fund and real-world asset onto a shared blockchain ledger.

"I think the biggest catalyst we're seeing right now is actually very much in motion and it's happening, which is capital markets are moving onchain," eToro CEO Yoni Assia told CNBC in an exclusive interview from the conference. He pointed to the SpaceX IPO as an example, which had derivatives trading on eToro before the IPO.

"First day of trading, we distributed it to our retail clients, and it started trading 24/7, and that's all because capital markets are now copying crypto markets and are sitting on the same rails," Assia said. "Crypto markets have always been 24/7, they never sleep, and now what we're seeing is that capital markets are copying crypto markets using the same technology so stocks are moving onchain, they're being tokenized."

Assia said that supportive regulation in the U.S. from agencies, including the Securities and Exchange Commission and the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, is also helping adoption of crypto.

He pointed to the CFTC earlier this month clearing the way for crypto-style perpetual futures tied to individual stocks to trade on regulated U.S. exchanges for the first time. Assia also highlighted the SEC's move to issue an order in September, creating a regulatory pathway for certain trading venues to trade tokenized representations of publicly traded U.S. stocks.

"Blockchain technology is now starting to power what's happening in capital markets," Assia told CNBC. "That is the one of the biggest changes we're going to see in capital markets for many, many years."

"We're talking about hundreds of trillions of dollars that are going to be tokenized and move onchain and at the same time, I do believe that will support prices of bitcoin and other blockchains as well," he continued.

The industry is also watching whether inflation will ease and geopolitical tensions will subside in the new year, which could lower broader macro risk currently hampering the industry.

Last month, Strategy's Michael Saylor said he thinks bitcoin's next bull market is already underway.

ARK Invest Chief Investment Officer Cathie Wood told CNBC the investment management firm's $1.5 million bull case price target for bitcoin by 2030 still stands. She also said that it wouldn't surprise her if bitcoin reached its all-time high again before the end of 2027.

"Our macro forecast has inflation breaking down considerably," Wood told CNBC in an exclusive interview at Cantor Convergence. "I think the elections have increased our confidence there because the war [in Iran], one way or the other, is going to end."

She added that "bitcoin has weathered so much bad news," including the $6.3 billion Binance hack and the "distraction" of AI which, "many people would say is sucking the oxygen away from bitcoin."

The maturation of the artificial intelligence trade could provide an opportunity for bitcoin to find new inflows.

Wood said that ARK Invest believes bitcoin has found its low and stressed that cybersecurity is "top of mind right now," which could provide yet another opportunity for crypto markets to rebound.

"I think if the Bitcoin and crypto world coalesces and makes investors feel that they've got their arms around security and actually understand that there might be more security issues in the traditional financial system, I think we're going to see a wave of support," Wood said.
